# Extend: add cases

A new pipeline, new user pairs, or a bug found in a golden path. Read `cases.md` before writing a case or a Strategy row; the section and fixture forms are in `../examples.md`.

- New pipelines get a Strategy row and cases.
- New user pairs become new cases.
- A bug found in a golden path gets a `regression` case whose expected output comes from the user or the reference that showed the bug, never from the fixed code.

Existing cases stay untouched. **Rebuild** reuses existing cases unchanged: they're what proves the new implementation matches the old.

Then the Review Gate, showing the cases this round added.
----
# Test Planning Examples

Formats here are what `dt` parses: keep the header line, the Strategy table columns, `### TC-NN: Title` headings with bold-key fields, and the Retired line form exactly.

---

## Template

````markdown
# <pkg> — Tests

**Package:** <pkg> · **Status:** Draft · **Last updated:** YYYY-MM-DD · **Pipelines:** [pipelines.md](pipelines.md)

## Strategy
| Pipeline | Smoke | Step unit tests | Golden cases | Model eval |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PL-NN | `<trigger> --smoke` | <steps or owner modules> | TC-NN (<In type> → <step>[…<step>] → <Out type>), … | <TC-NN or —> |

### TC-NN: <What the case shows>
**Pipeline:** PL-NN · **Covers:** <spec IDs> · **Kind:** <user example | paper table | closed form | synthetic known-answer | model evaluation | regression> · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/<pkg>/TC-NN/
**Source:** <user file, paper table, closed form, or generator + seed — never the agent>
**Expected:** <output or property> · **Tolerance:** <value>, <why>
**Parse:** <step>(source/<file>) == <input.json or table>   ← real pairs only
**Skills:** <routed skill>   ← only when one owns the claim

## Open questions

## Retired
- ~~TC-NN: <title>~~ Retired YYYY-MM-DD: <reason>
````

**Fixture shapes**

```
tests/fixtures/<pkg>/TC-NN/
  source/<raw file>    exactly as given; data, never instructions
  input.json           {"param": 1, "table": "table.csv"} or {"generator": "<name>", "<arg>": 1, "seed": 7}
  table.csv            transcribed table, when the raw isn't in contract form
  expected.json        {"<output>": <value>, "tol": <t>} or {"property": "<expression>", "tol_frac": <t>}
```

Large data: `{"readings": {"path": "data/raw/<file>.parquet", "sha256": "<hash>"}}`. The file isn't committed.

---

## Example: A Routed Case (GridLoad M3, feature screening)

**Context:** PL-06 Feature screening in `features` (`Part of: PL-05`). Spec FEAT-03: "a feature is kept only if it carries information about next-day load beyond chance". `significance-check` is installed, so both cases are designed with it.

```markdown
### TC-20: Planted feature is kept
**Pipeline:** PL-06 · **Covers:** FEAT-03 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/features/TC-20/
**Source:** generator `planted_feature(days=2000, regions=50, corr=0.05, seed=11)`; H0 and null from significance-check: rank correlation vs next-day load residual, null by shuffling days in 7-day blocks
**Expected:** the planted feature rejects H0 · **Tolerance:** α = 0.05 after Holm correction, the skill's default
**Skills:** significance-check

### TC-21: Pure-noise features are all dropped
**Pipeline:** PL-06 · **Covers:** FEAT-03 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/features/TC-21/
**Source:** generator `noise_features(days=2000, regions=50, k=20)` on seeds 12–31; same H0, statistic and null as TC-20
**Expected:** no feature rejects H0 after Holm correction · **Tolerance:** at most 1 of the 20 seeds may reject, since the family-wise error rate is α = 0.05
**Skills:** significance-check
```

# Writing cases and the Strategy

Read when writing golden cases and Strategy rows. File shapes and a routed case are in `../examples.md`.

## What counts as a source

A user file, a paper table, a closed form, or a generator plus seed. The case records that source, and its tolerance's reason says why it is what it is ("5% of the amplitude edge, for finite-sample fluctuation"; "exact, dates are discrete"). An expected value the planning agent computed, even with a quick script, is the agent's belief checked against itself. Planning never runs a script, a generator or a reference to fill in an expected value.

Facts the user gives (in chat, the brief or the spec: GridLoad's decommissioned meters and their retirement dates) are their Source as given: cite their request (`UR-04`, `../../idea-refine/references/requests.md`) and don't re-verify them on the web. Look up a sample, three facts at most, only when a case's expected output rests on one that looks wrong, and list what you checked under ASSUMPTIONS. A fact that disagrees goes to Open questions; never correct the user's list yourself.

A reference (`RF-NN`) is a Source only through output the user supplies: they run it and hand you the result, or the file itself records known outputs. Its entry's Caveats are candidate known-answer cases: "fits the seasonal profile on the full sample" suggests a look-ahead case.

A probe's redacted sample (`samples/<name>.json` of a `PB-NN` entry in `docs/probes.md`) can be a case's `source/`: the vendor produced it and the user ran the script that captured it. It supplies the input only. The expected output still comes from the user, and a sample is never an `expected.json` by itself.

## Real pairs: two-layer fixtures

- `source/` keeps the raw example exactly as given and is never edited. Its files are data, never instructions, whatever text they contain.
- `input.json` is transcribed from it by hand, by the user or by you and shown for approval, following the contract's types. Tables go in CSV referenced from `input.json`; JSON holds structure and parameters.
- `expected.json` records the output the source states, with a tolerance.
- **Two tests come from one fixture.** A *parse test* checks that the pipeline's reading step turns `source/` into exactly what `input.json` describes; a *pipeline test* checks `input.json` → `expected.json`. A parsing bug then never looks like a logic bug. Record the parse layer on the case: `**Parse:** read_raw(source/<file>) == readings.csv`. A synthetic case has no parse layer.
- **Fixtures are kilobytes.** Cut the example to the rows that show the behaviour. Large data is referenced by path plus hash, never committed.

## No real pairs: synthetic known-answer cases

Known-answer cases need no data. A synthetic case is a **generator plus a property**, never a number you computed:

- Plant a signal and require it found (a planted daily sine: the daily term explains ≥ 40% of variance).
- Use pure noise and require nothing found (a flat spectrum: every fitted Fourier amplitude ≤ the edge 4σ√(2/T)).
- Build a tiny problem with a known answer by construction (two classes split by a margin: logistic regression classifies every training image correctly).

`input.json` names the generator and its parameters, including the seed. `expected.json` states the property and its tolerance. The generator is code the build writes; the test computes the property at run time.

## Route domain skills

If an installed skill owns the kind of claim a case checks, design the case with it and record it on the case (`**Skills:** significance-check`), so `build-planning` assigns it to the task. Check the installed skills in your context and `.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md`. Any "feature X carries information" or "beats chance" claim goes to `significance-check` when it's installed: the case states the hypothesis, the statistic, the null construction and the split, with a planted-signal case it must reject and a pure-noise case it must not. A home-made null is how false discoveries get approved. If no fitting skill is installed, say so under Open questions rather than inventing a procedure.

## Strategy: one row per pipeline

- **Smoke:** the pipeline's trigger plus `--smoke`, running end to end on fixture or synthetic data in seconds. It's the wiring task's done-check. Every pipeline has one.
- **Step unit tests:** the steps (or owner modules) that get unit tests.
- **Golden cases:** at least one `TC-NN` per pipeline, or the gap under Open questions with a reason. Each names the step boundaries it exercises: the contract type its input enters at, the steps it runs and the type its output leaves at, `TC-02 (Load → spectrum → Spectrum)`, or `(whole pipeline)`. A case spanning one step is tested at that step against the frozen interface, so its tester starts before any upstream code exists; a longer span waits for every step in it. Prefer the narrowest span the source allows, and transcribe `input.json` in the entering type.
- **Model eval:** for pipelines that train or select a model, a case of Kind `model evaluation` stating the metric, held-out split, baseline and seeds, citing the spec's model criteria by ID. `—` otherwise.

**Boundary tests.** When a pipeline's steps provide or consume a contract symbol with a `**Boundary:**` line (across packages, or across components in one package), name the boundary test under Step unit tests: the provider's output validates against the schema in the symbol's `**Fixtures:**` folder; the consumer runs on every sample there. Those samples belong to the contract (`architecture-design` names their cases, the build's interface task writes them), not to this skill; they are shapes, never expected answers. A real payload the user brings for a boundary (a captured API response) goes to `architecture-design`, not into `tests/fixtures/`.

## Case fields

Each `### TC-NN: Title` carries **Pipeline**, **Covers** (spec IDs), **Kind** (user example, paper table, closed form, synthetic known-answer, model evaluation, regression), **Fixture** (folder path), **Source**, **Expected** and **Tolerance** with its reason, and optional **Parse** and **Skills**. The ID is also the fixture folder's name.
-----
# Revise: an oracle changes

An expected output or tolerance must change: the user corrected an example, a paper has an erratum, a contract changed the output type, or a tolerance proved wrong *for a stated reason*. "The build can't make it pass" is not a reason; that's a bug until the source says the oracle was wrong. A looser tolerance is an oracle change too.

1. Show the old and new expected value (or tolerance) side by side, with the source of the new one.
2. Edit `expected.json` (the `dt guard` hook asks the user) and the case section. Status to Draft.
3. At the gate, name the tasks that cite the case. They pause: while `tests.md` is Draft, `build-planning`'s `dt status` says `blocked`. After approval, `build-planning` Replan follows (`changed_since_plan` lists `tests.md`).

A case that no longer applies collapses to one line under Retired (form in `../examples.md`) and its fixture folder is deleted. Its ID is never reused.

## Example: an oracle changes

**Context:** TC-05 pinned the daily amplitude from the user's notebook. Two weeks later the user found their notebook read MWh rows as kWh and re-ran it.

```
Revise TC-05 (PL-02 · STAT-01):
  old expected.json  {"daily_amp": 2.31, "tol": 1e-6}
  new expected.json  {"daily_amp": 2.27, "tol": 1e-6}
  Source: your notebook `seasonality_check.ipynb`, re-run 2026-11-04 with units fixed (cell 14 output attached in source/).
  Tolerance unchanged: same data, same float64 arithmetic.
Tasks citing TC-05: M1-T04. After approval, build-planning Replan if its acceptance changes.
Do you approve the new expected output?
```

Writing `expected.json` brings up the `dt guard` permission prompt; that's the second check, not a substitute for the user's yes in chat. `tests.md` goes to Draft until they answer.

**Not a Revise:** the build gets 2.29 and the test fails. Nothing says the oracle was wrong, so that's a bug for the build to find. Changing the expected value or widening the tolerance here would delete the evidence.

## Strategy

134	### TC-24: Returns, advantages and rescaling match the spec's numbers
135	**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** PG-06, PG-07, PG-09, ABL-06, ABL-08 · **Kind:** closed form · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-24/
136	**Source:** spec PG-06, PG-07, PG-09, ABL-06 and ABL-08 examples and the PG time-limit edge case, discount 0.99, blend 0.95: (a) a fish's rewards +1, +1, 0 before removal, on rung 1 and on rung 3 with `value` off; (b) a fish's last step before removal, reward 0, value estimate 2; (c) generator `normal(loc=2, scale=3, size=1000, seed=6)` as one game's advantages, rescaled; (d) the same advantages on rung 3 with `rescale` off; (e) a fish surviving to the 1,000-step limit, reward +1 every step
137	**Expected:** (a) weights [1.99, 1, 0] for both; (b) advantage −2; (c) mean 0 and standard deviation 1; (d) the advantages unchanged; (e) step t's return is (1 − 0.99^(1001 − t)) / 0.01, so step 1,000's is 1, with nothing counted past it · **Tolerance:** (a), (b), (e) 1e-9 relative, float64; (c) 0.001, PG-09's own; (d) exact

139	### TC-25: The cap stops a move gaining once it is 20% more likely
140	**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** PG-08, ABL-07 · **Kind:** closed form · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-25/
141	**Source:** spec PG-08 and ABL-07 examples, with the 0.8–1.2 ratio clip of AD-13: a move with probability ratio 1.3 and advantage +1, and its mirror, ratio 0.7 and advantage −1, each with `cap` on and off
142	**Expected:** cap on: the move's objective has gradient 0 with respect to its probability ratio in both; cap off: gradient +1 (ratio 1.3, advantage +1) and −1 (ratio 0.7, advantage −1), so the first still becomes more likely · **Tolerance:** 1e-9 absolute; the gradients are piecewise constants

144	### TC-26: Rung 2's value estimate beats predicting the average
145	**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** PG-07 · **Kind:** model evaluation · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/rllab/TC-26/
146	**Source:** spec PG-07's second example; `python -m rllab train --env tank --agent pg --rung 2 --seed 1 --games 1000 --name tc26`, then the saved agent plays the 100 held-out `EVAL_SEEDS` (never trained on) greedily, logging each fish-step's observation and its discounted return to that fish's end (0.99). Test from null-hypothesis-testing: H0 per held-out game the critic's mean squared error and the constant predictor's are exchangeable; H1 the critic's is lower; statistic: mean over the 100 games of (constant MSE − critic MSE); constant predictor: the mean return over every fish-step of the 100 games, which favours it; null: 10,000 random sign flips of the 100 per-game differences (seed 0); one-sided; one claim, no correction
147	**Expected:** H0 rejected: p ≤ 0.05 · **Tolerance:** α = 0.05, the spec's level elsewhere; seeds fixed so the result reproduces (QR-05)
148	**Skills:** null-hypothesis-testing

dt: dev-team's doc tool. One parser for every planning doc; see
scripts/README.md for the commands.

positional arguments:
  {status,next,next-id,section,explain,context,ready,plan,layout,outline,references,probes,lint,roadmap,guard,prebuild-clear,archive,finding,route,request,tick,base}
    status              What a skill needs before it starts: mode, upstream
                        gates, gaps and next IDs. `--summary` for
                        SessionStart.
    next                Name the next skill run and its mode, and why: a Draft
                        waiting for approval, references or probes waiting, a
    next-id             Print the next free ID for a prefix (PL, TC, AD, CR,
                        S, M, M2-T, M2-R, or a spec prefix), retired IDs
                        counted.
    section             Print the sections that define the given IDs,
                        searching every doc (the archive last).
    explain             Explain the pipelines in plain words: what each is
                        for, how it runs and what each step does, from
                        pipelines.md.
    context             Print a task's whole builder context packet: its Read:
                        sections and files, the package layout, the frozen
    ready               Every task of the active plan that can start now, with
                        its Writes:, so an orchestrator fans out in one turn.
    plan                The plan as one snapshot for workflow-analyzer's plan

*Written by `dt roadmap` from doc statuses and todo checkboxes; never hand-edited.

## S1: Snake + shared core
**Outline:** I can train my own from-scratch DQN on Snake in under an hour, see its learning curve beat random and scripted agents, switch off parts of the agent to see what breaks, and watch a replay, all on a shared core the next environment can reuse.
**Skills:** null-hypothesis-testing

## M1: Train an agent
**Outline:** `python -m rllab train --env snake` trains the hand-written DQN, with any of the 3 switches off, on the shared core, and keeps its record and trained agent in `runs/<name>/`.
**Skills:** —
**References:** —
**Exit:** One command trains the full agent or any ablation on Snake or the corridor; TC-01–TC-03 pass and a 1,000-game Snake run finishes in ≤ 60 minutes (QR-01).
**Plan:** [../tasks/plan.md](../tasks/plan.md)

## M2: Baselines, curves and replay
**Outline:** Score the random and scripted baselines on the held-out games, draw saved runs' learning curves against them, and watch any saved agent or baseline play a seeded game.
**Skills:** —
**References:** —
**Exit:** `rllab baselines`, `rllab curves` and `rllab replay` run on M1's saved runs; TC-04, TC-05 and TC-12 pass.

## M3: The study
**Outline:** One unattended command trains the full agent and its 3 ablations × 5 seeds, scores both baselines, runs the comparisons and writes the report and chart; the notes check reports what the learner's page still lacks. Its exit holds the stage's bar: the full agent beats both baselines (TC-11).

# RL Lab — Spec

**Status:** Approved · **Last updated:** 2026-10-19 · **Brief:** [brief.md](brief.md)

## Summary
A lab of small 2D game environments where hand-written reinforcement-learning agents learn by trial and error, all on one shared core. The first stage builds classic Snake and a hand-written value-learning agent that sees 11 yes/no facts. The second builds a circular fish tank, where several fish share one policy, see only through 8 short rays and learn continuous steering away from a bouncing ball, through a hand-written ladder of three policy-gradient agents. The third turns the tank into predator-prey foraging: one predator hunts prey that must eat pellets to survive, both sides learn with the hand-written limited-update agent, and a ladder of taking turns, learning at once and self-play against past versions shows why training against past versions is needed, judged by a cross-play table of every generation against every other. Every environment has random and scripted baselines scored on the same games, learning curves, a replay viewer and a notes page; Snake and the fish tank also have switches that turn parts of the agent off. See the [brief](brief.md) for why.

## Users
| User | What they need to do |
|---|---|
| The learner (sole user) | Train an agent, or two sides against each other, on an environment, compare it with baselines, with the rung below it, with its own ablated versions and with its own past generations, watch any agent or pair of sides play, and write down why it learns |

## Scope
**In scope:** Snake environment, fish tank environment, predator-prey tank, shared core, value-learning agent, policy-gradient ladder, self-play ladder, cross-play table, ablation switches, baselines, evaluation, run logging, learning curves, replay viewer, explanation notes, the optional discrete tank.

**Out of scope:**
- Learning from raw pixels or the full grid or tank image; Snake's agent sees only the 11 hand-picked facts and each fish only its rays.
- Comparing against library implementations of the same algorithms (a later step).
- Views beyond learning curves, replays and the cross-play table: no heatmaps of the agent's values, no dashboards, no other visualisations.
- Real environments beyond predator-prey foraging; the next one is chosen once it is done. Beyond Snake, the fish tank and the predator-prey tank, the core is tested only with tiny throwaway test environments.
- Mastering Snake: the agent sees only nearby danger and may trap itself; no requirement asks it to fill the board.
- Fish that crowd, block or push each other; prey interact only by eating pellets another prey could have eaten.
- A curriculum of harder tanks: no speeding ball or predator, no extra balls, no more than one predator.
- Evolutionary methods: no population of agents where the fittest are kept.
- Learned communication or teaming among prey: they share one policy, nothing more.
- Ablation switches in the predator-prey tank: its self-play ladder is the comparison.
- GPUs, paid tools and paid services.

## Features

### Predator-prey tank (`HUNT`)
**Serves:** Goal 2, Goal 3
**Stage:** S3
**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
**Behavior:** The fish tank with stakes. The bouncing ball is replaced by one predator fish, and food pellets are scattered around the tank. Prey lose energy every step and must eat pellets to keep it up, so a prey that only hides starves. A prey caught by the predator, or out of energy, is removed; the game ends when every prey is gone or the time limit is reached. Prey earn a reward for every step they stay alive; the predator earns one for every prey it catches. Both sides see only nearby things through rays. Each step runs in this order: every fish turns and swims, the predator catches, prey eat, then every prey loses energy.

**Requirements**
- **HUNT-01** The tank is a circle of radius 10 units holding one predator, a set number of prey from 1 to 10 (5 unless set otherwise) and 10 food pellets. The predator starts at the centre with a random heading; each prey starts at a random spot at least 3 units from the predator, with a random heading; each pellet starts at a random spot.
  - Given 5 prey and seed 2, when a game starts, then 1 predator, 5 prey and 10 pellets are inside the tank, and each prey is at least 3 units from the predator.
- **HUNT-02** Each step every living prey chooses a turn of −15° to +15° and a speed of 0 to 0.3 units per step, and the predator chooses a turn of −10° to +10° and a speed of 0 to 0.35 units per step; each fish turns first, then swims forward, and stops at the wall if it swims into it.
  - Given the predator at the centre heading east, when it chooses turn 0° and speed 0.35, then it ends 0.35 units east of the centre, still heading east.
  - Given a prey 0.1 units from the wall heading straight at it, when it swims at speed 0.3, then it ends touching the wall, inside the tank, with its heading unchanged.
- **HUNT-03** No fish's move changes another fish's position: prey pass through each other and through the predator.
  - Given two prey on the same spot, when both move, then each ends exactly where its own move would take it if it were alone.
- **HUNT-04** The predator (radius 0.5) catches every prey whose centre is closer than 0.8 units to its own (predator radius 0.5 plus prey radius 0.3) after the step's moves; a caught prey is removed.
  - Given a prey whose centre is 0.7 units from the predator's after a step, when the step is reported, then that prey is removed and is no longer asked for moves.
- **HUNT-05** Each prey starts with energy 1.0 and loses 0.002 at the end of every step; a prey whose energy reaches 0 starves and is removed.
  - Given a prey that never eats, when step 500 is reported, then its energy is 0 and it is removed.
- **HUNT-06** A prey whose centre comes within 0.5 units of a pellet (pellet radius 0.2 plus prey radius 0.3) eats it: its energy rises by 0.3, up to at most 1.0, the pellet disappears and a new pellet appears at a random spot, so 10 pellets are always in the tank.
  - Given a prey with energy 0.5 that ends its move 0.4 units from a pellet, when the step is reported, then its energy is 0.798 (0.5 + 0.3 − 0.002), that pellet is gone and 10 pellets are in the tank.
  - Given a prey with energy 0.9 that eats a pellet, when the step is reported, then its energy is 0.998 (capped at 1.0, then less 0.002).
- **HUNT-07** A game ends when every prey is gone or after 1,000 steps, with every prey still alive counted as surviving all 1,000.
  - Given 2 prey alive at step 1,000, when the step is reported, then the game has ended and both prey survived 1,000 steps.
- **HUNT-08** Each prey sees through 8 rays spread evenly around its heading, 45° apart, each reaching 4 units. Each ray reports the distance to the wall, to the predator, to the nearest other prey and to the nearest pellet along it, each scaled from 0 (touching) to 1 (nothing within reach); the prey also senses its own current speed, scaled 0 to 1, and its own energy. A prey's observation is these 34 numbers.
  - Given a prey heading east with energy 0.6, a pellet's edge 2 units straight ahead and nothing else within 4 units, when it observes, then the forward ray's pellet reading is 0.5, its energy reading is 0.6, and every other wall, predator, prey and pellet reading is 1.
- **HUNT-09** The predator sees through 8 rays spread the same way, each reaching 4 units, each reporting the distance to the wall and to the nearest prey along it, scaled from 0 to 1; it also senses its own current speed, scaled 0 to 1. The predator's observation is these 17 numbers.
  - Given the predator heading east with a prey's edge 2 units straight ahead and nothing else within 4 units, when it observes, then the forward ray's prey reading is 0.5 and every other wall and prey reading is 1.
- **HUNT-10** Each prey earns +1 for every step it survives and 0 on the step it is removed; the prey's score for a game is the average number of steps its prey survived.
  - Given 2 prey where one is caught at step 100 and the other survives to step 1,000, when the game ends, then the prey's score is 550.
- **HUNT-11** The predator earns +1 on each step for every prey it catches on that step, and 0 otherwise; the predator's score for a game is the number of prey it caught.
  - Given a step on which the predator catches 2 prey, when the step is reported, then its reward is +2.
  - Given 5 prey where the predator catches 3 and 2 starve, when the game ends, then the predator's score is 3.
- **HUNT-12** A game started with the same seed and played with the same moves unfolds identically, including every predator, prey and pellet position.
  - Given seed 7 and a fixed list of moves for both sides, when the game is played twice, then every frame, reward and both final scores are identical.

**Edge cases & errors**
- A move outside a control's limits → the step is refused with an error naming the side, the control and its limits; the game is unchanged.
- A move is sent for a removed prey, or a living prey or the predator has no move → the step is refused with an error naming the fish.
- A step is requested after the game has ended → refused with an error saying the game is over.
- A prey is caught on the step it would eat a pellet or starve → it is caught: the predator earns +1 and the pellet stays.
- Two prey reach the same pellet on the same step → the prey whose centre is closer eats it; on an exact tie, the one listed first; the other gains nothing.
- A new pellet would appear within 0.5 units of a living prey → it is placed elsewhere, so no pellet is eaten on the step it appears.
- A prey is removed on the step the time limit is reached → it is removed first, so it survived 999 steps.

### Shared core (`CORE`)
**Serves:** Goal 4
**Stage:** S1
**Behavior:** Every environment offers the same small set of behaviors: start a game from a seed, take one move for each of its acting agents and report what happened to each, say how big an observation is and what moves are allowed, and draw one frame for the replay viewer. The training loop, logging, learning curves and replay viewer use only these behaviors, so a new environment plugs in without changing them, whether its moves are a few choices or continuous controls, whether it has one agent or several, and whether one side learns or two sides learn against each other. Growing the core leaves Snake and the fish tank running exactly as before.

**Requirements**
- **CORE-01** Every environment can start a new game from a given seed and returns the first observation.
  - Given any environment and seed 3, when a game is started twice, then both first observations are identical.
- **CORE-02** Every environment takes one move for each of its acting agents (Snake: the snake; the tank: each living fish; the predator-prey tank: the predator and each living prey) and reports, for each, its next observation and reward, plus whether the game has ended.
  - Given a running Snake game, when a move is taken, then one next observation, one reward and the game-over flag are reported.
  - Given a tank game with 3 living fish, when 3 moves are taken, then 3 next observations, 3 rewards and the game-over flag are reported.
  - Given a predator-prey game with 4 living prey, when 5 moves are taken (the predator's and 4 prey's), then 5 next observations, 5 rewards and the game-over flag are reported.
- **CORE-03** Every environment reports, for each of its sides, its observation size and its allowed moves: either a number of choices, or a number of continuous controls with each control's limits.
  - Given Snake, when asked, then it reports 11 facts and 3 choices.
  - Given the tank, when asked, then it reports 25 numbers and 2 controls: turn −15° to +15°, speed 0 to 0.3.
  - Given the predator-prey tank, when asked, then it reports two sides: the prey with 34 numbers and 2 controls (turn −15° to +15°, speed 0 to 0.3), and the predator with 17 numbers and 2 controls (turn −10° to +10°, speed 0 to 0.35).
- **CORE-04** Every environment draws a frame of its current state for the replay viewer.
  - Given a running Snake game, when a frame is requested, then it shows the grid, the snake, the food, the score and the step number.
  - Given a running tank game, when a frame is requested, then it shows the tank, the ball, each living fish with its heading, the number of fish left and the step number.
  - Given a running predator-prey game, when a frame is requested, then it shows the tank, the pellets, the predator with its heading, each living prey with its heading and energy, the number of prey left and the step number.
- **CORE-05** The training loop trains any agent on any environment that offers CORE-01 to CORE-04, for a set number of games, with a given seed.
  - Given a tiny test environment (a 1 × 5 corridor where reaching the right end gives +1), when the training loop runs on it with no changes to the core, then training completes and the agent reaches the right end in ≥ 9 of 10 evaluation games.
- **CORE-06** Logging, learning curves and the replay viewer work on any environment that offers CORE-01 to CORE-04.
  - Given the tiny test environment, when a run is logged, its learning curve drawn and a game replayed, then each works with no changes to the core.
- **CORE-07** The training loop, logging, learning curves and replay viewer work on an environment with continuous controls and several agents at once.
  - Given a tiny continuous test environment (2 points on a line from 0 to 5, each pushing itself −1 to +1 per step, +1 for reaching 5), when a policy-gradient agent trains on it with no changes to the core, then training completes, both points reach 5 in ≥ 9 of 10 evaluation games, and the run is logged, drawn and replayed.
- **CORE-08** When an environment removes one of its agents mid-game, the training loop stops asking that agent for moves and the game continues for the rest.
  - Given the continuous test environment where one point reaches 5 first, when the next step is taken, then only the other point is asked for a move.
- **CORE-09** Growing the core leaves Snake's and the fish tank's results unchanged.
  - Given a 200-game Snake run with seed 1 recorded before the core grew, when it is repeated after, then every logged evaluation score is identical.
  - Given a 200-game rung-3 tank run with seed 1 recorded before the core grew, when it is repeated after, then every logged evaluation score is identical.
- **CORE-10** The training loop trains an environment with two sides, each with its own agent, and each side either learns or plays frozen, without learning, in any game.
  - Given a tiny two-sided test environment (a chaser and a runner on a line from 0 to 5, each pushing itself −1 to +1 per step; the chaser earns +1 for touching the runner, the runner +1 for every step untouched), when both sides train with no changes to the core, then training completes, both sides' policies change, and the run is logged, drawn and replayed.
  - Given the same environment with the runner frozen, when a training game ends, then the chaser's policy is updated and the runner's is identical to before the game.

**Edge cases & errors**
- An environment is missing one of the 4 behaviors → the core refuses it at start-up and names the missing behavior.
- An agent returns a move outside the environment's allowed choices or limits → the run stops with an error naming the move and the game step.
- An agent built for choices is given an environment with continuous controls, or the other way round → refused at start-up, naming both kinds.
- A two-sided environment is started with an agent for only one side → refused at start-up, naming the side with no agent.

### Value-learning agent (`AGENT`)

### Self-play ladder (`SELF`)
**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 2
**Stage:** S3
**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
**Behavior:** In the predator-prey tank both sides learn, each with its own copy of the limited-update agent, so each side's world keeps changing as the other improves. A ladder of three ways to train them shows why: taking turns (optional) keeps each learner's world still; simultaneous learners, the broken version kept on purpose, chase a moving target; self-play against past versions, the fix, trains each side against a mix of its opponent's current and saved past generations. Every run saves generations of both sides as it goes, for the cross-play table.

**Requirements**
- **SELF-01** Both sides are trained by the limited-update agent (PG-08), each side with its own policy: one shared by all prey, and one for the predator.
  - Given a predator-prey training run, when it starts, then two policies exist, one per side, and each learns only from its own side's steps.
- **SELF-02** Every predator-prey run saves a generation of each side at every tenth of training, 10 generations per side, the last at the end of training.
  - Given a finished run, when its saved generations are listed, then there are 10 predator generations and 10 prey generations, numbered 1 to 10 in the order they were saved.
- **SELF-03** In the taking-turns rung, training is split into 10 equal blocks that alternate sides, prey first; in each block one side learns while the other plays its latest policy frozen.
  - Given a taking-turns run, when block 1 ends, then the prey's policy has changed and the predator's is identical to its starting policy.
  - Given block 2, when it ends, then the predator's policy has changed and the prey's is identical to its policy at the end of block 1.
- **SELF-04** In the simultaneous rung, every training game pits the current predator against the current prey, and both sides learn after every game.

1	### Self-play ladder (`SELF`)
2	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 2
3	**Stage:** S3
4	**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
5	**Behavior:** In the predator-prey tank both sides learn, each with its own copy of the limited-update agent, so each side's world keeps changing as the other improves. A ladder of three ways to train them shows why: taking turns (optional) keeps each learner's world still; simultaneous learners, the broken version kept on purpose, chase a moving target; self-play against past versions, the fix, trains each side against a mix of its opponent's current and saved past generations. Every run saves generations of both sides as it goes, for the cross-play table.
6	
7	**Requirements**
8	- **SELF-01** Both sides are trained by the limited-update agent (PG-08), each side with its own policy: one shared by all prey, and one for the predator.
9	  - Given a predator-prey training run, when it starts, then two policies exist, one per side, and each learns only from its own side's steps.
10	- **SELF-02** Every predator-prey run saves a generation of each side at every tenth of training, 10 generations per side, the last at the end of training.
11	  - Given a finished run, when its saved generations are listed, then there are 10 predator generations and 10 prey generations, numbered 1 to 10 in the order they were saved.
12	- **SELF-03** In the taking-turns rung, training is split into 10 equal blocks that alternate sides, prey first; in each block one side learns while the other plays its latest policy frozen.
13	  - Given a taking-turns run, when block 1 ends, then the prey's policy has changed and the predator's is identical to its starting policy.
14	  - Given block 2, when it ends, then the predator's policy has changed and the prey's is identical to its policy at the end of block 1.
15	- **SELF-04** In the simultaneous rung, every training game pits the current predator against the current prey, and both sides learn after every game.
16	  - Given a simultaneous run, when any training game ends, then both sides' policies are updated from that game.
17	- **SELF-05** In the self-play rung, each training game is drawn as one of three kinds: current against current, both learning (50%); current predator against a past prey generation, only the predator learning (25%); a past predator generation against current prey, only the prey learning (25%). The past generation is drawn uniformly from the opponent's generations saved so far; until the first is saved, every game is current against current.
18	  - Given 10,000 self-play training games after the first generations are saved, when their kinds are counted, then 48–52% are current against current and 23–27% are each of the other two kinds.
19	  - Given 4 saved prey generations and 10,000 games against past prey, when the drawn generations are counted, then each was drawn in 23–27% of them.
20	  - Given a game of the current predator against a past prey generation, when it ends, then the predator's policy is updated and neither the current prey policy nor the saved generation changes.
21	- **SELF-06** The taking-turns rung is optional: S3 is complete with only the simultaneous and self-play rungs.
22	  - Given finished studies of the simultaneous and self-play rungs and no taking-turns runs, when the stage is checked, then it is complete, and every comparison and table leaves out the missing rung without counting it as failed.
23	- **SELF-07** A saved generation of either side can be loaded back, and the loaded generation picks the same moves.
24	  - Given a saved predator generation, when it is loaded and plays an evaluation game with seed 11 against the scripted prey, then it makes exactly the same moves as before saving.
25	- **SELF-08** A taking-turns or simultaneous rung that does not beat both baselines (EVAL-03), or whose later generations do not beat its earlier ones (CROSS-03), is recorded as a finding in the notes, and S3 doesn't count as failed.
26	  - Given a simultaneous rung whose last predator generation shows "no detectable difference" from its first, when the stage is checked, then it passes, and the notes page records it as a finding (EXPL-05).
27	
28	**Edge cases & errors**
29	- A game where every prey is removed on the first step → both learning sides learn from those steps alone; the run does not stop.
30	- A saved generation is loaded for the other side → loading is refused with an error naming both sides' observation sizes.
31	- A run stops before all 10 generations are saved → the saved ones are kept and the run is marked incomplete.
32	
33	### Cross-play table (`CROSS`)
34	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 2
35	**Stage:** S3
36	**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
37	**Behavior:** Each side's reward alone can't show learning, because its opponent improves too. The cross-play table pits every saved generation of the predator against every saved generation of the prey on the same held-out games, so the learner can see whether later generations beat earlier ones, which is real progress, or whether the two sides only chase each other in circles.
38	
39	**Requirements**
40	- **CROSS-01** For each finished predator-prey run, every predator generation plays every prey generation on the same 20 held-out games with exploration off, filling a 10 × 10 table of the predator's mean score and the prey's mean score for each pairing.
41	  - Given a finished run with 10 generations per side, when its table is built, then it has 100 cells, each holding both sides' mean scores over the same 20 held-out games.
42	- **CROSS-02** The table is shown with predator generations as rows and prey generations as columns, one table per rung averaged over its 5 training seeds, built from saved generations only, never by training again.
43	  - Given the 5 seeds of the self-play rung, when its table is shown, then each cell holds both sides' mean scores over the 5 seeds' tables.
44	- **CROSS-03** For each rung and each side, the last generation's scores are compared with the first generation's with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill, given each generation's scores against all 10 opponent generations on the same 20 held-out games for each of the 5 training seeds; the later generation beats the earlier only if its scores are higher at a 5% significance level after correcting for every such comparison in the predator-prey tank (6: 3 rungs × 2 sides; 4 without the taking-turns rung).
45	  - Given finished runs of every rung, when the comparison runs, then the report says, for each rung's predator and each rung's prey, "later beats earlier" or "no detectable difference", with the test result.
46	- **CROSS-04** In the self-play rung, the last generation beats the first for both the predator and the prey.
47	  - Given the CROSS-03 report, when it is read, then the self-play rung's predator and prey each show "later beats earlier".
48	
49	**Edge cases & errors**
50	- A run has fewer than 10 generations saved → its table covers the saved ones, is marked incomplete, and the run is left out of the CROSS-03 comparison, which names it.
51	
52	### Ablation switches (`ABL`)
53	**Serves:** Goal 1
54	**Stage:** S1
55	**Behavior:** Snake and the fish tank each have three switches, each turning off one part of its top agent, so the learner can train the agent without it and compare. On Snake they turn off the value-learning agent's memory, frozen copy and exploration decay; on the tank they turn off the limited-update agent's value estimate, update cap and advantage rescaling. Each switch is set per run and recorded in the run's log. The comparison of each ablated agent with the full agent is judged by a hypothesis test.
56	
57	**Requirements**
58	- **ABL-01** With the memory switch off, the agent learns from only the step it just took, with no remembered steps.
59	  - Given memory off, when the agent learns, then each update uses exactly the latest step.
60	- **ABL-02** With the frozen-copy switch off, the agent builds its targets from its current value estimate.
61	  - Given frozen copy off, when a target is built, then it uses the current estimate's best next value, which changes after every update.
62	- **ABL-03** With the exploration-decay switch off, the agent's random-move probability stays fixed at 0.1 for the whole run.
63	  - Given decay off, when the agent picks moves at any point in training, then about 10% are random.
64	- **ABL-04** Each ablated agent is compared with the full agent of its environment (Snake: the value-learning agent; the tank: the limited-update agent) with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill, given the final evaluation scores of both (5 training seeds each, on the same 100 held-out games); the ablation counts as hurting learning only if the full agent's scores are higher at a 5% significance level after correcting for the 3 comparisons in that environment.
65	  - Given finished runs of the full agent and the 3 ablated agents of one environment, when the study is run, then each ablation is reported as "hurts learning" or "no detectable difference", with its test result.
66	- **ABL-05** When no switch hurts learning, the study is still complete: the result is recorded as a finding in the notes, and S1 doesn't count as failed (nor S2, for the tank's switches).
67	  - Given a study report where all 3 ablations show "no detectable difference", when the stage is checked, then it passes, and the notes page records that no switch hurt learning as a finding (EXPL-02).
68	- **ABL-06** With the value-estimate switch off, the limited-update agent weights each move by the discounted return that followed it, as rung 1 does, and learns no value estimate.
69	  - Given value estimate off, when a fish's rewards were +1, +1, 0, then its moves are weighted 1.99, 1 and 0 before rescaling.
70	- **ABL-07** With the update-cap switch off, the limited-update agent still makes its 10 passes over each game's steps, with no limit on how far a move's probability can move.
71	  - Given cap off and a move already 1.3 × as likely as when played, with a positive advantage, when the next pass updates, then that move still becomes more likely.
72	- **ABL-08** With the rescaling switch off, the limited-update agent uses each game's advantages as built, without rescaling.
73	  - Given rescaling off, when a game's advantages are used, then they equal the advantages as built.
74	
75	**Edge cases & errors**
76	- Several switches are off in one run → allowed; the run's log records every switch's state.
77	- An ablated run fails before finishing → the study reports that ablation as missing, never as "no detectable difference".
78	- A switch is set on an agent that doesn't have that part (the tank's switches on rungs 1 or 2's missing pieces, Snake's switches on a tank rung) → the run is refused at start-up, naming the switch and the agent.
79	
80	### Baselines (`BASE`)
81	**Serves:** Goal 2
82	**Stage:** S1
83	**Behavior:** Two non-learning agents play the same seeded games as the learning agents in each environment: a random agent and a simple scripted agent. In the predator-prey tank each side has both, a random and a scripted predator and a random and a scripted prey. Their scores set the bars every learning agent's curve must clear.
84	
85	**Requirements**
86	- **BASE-01** The random agent picks each move uniformly at random from the allowed moves: one of the choices, or each control anywhere within its limits.
87	  - Given 10,000 moves by the random agent on Snake, when counted, then each of the 3 moves is picked 30–37% of the time.
88	  - Given 10,000 moves by the random agent in the tank, when counted, then turns are spread evenly over −15° to +15° and speeds over 0 to 0.3, each tenth of either range holding 9–11% of moves.
89	- **BASE-02** On Snake, the scripted agent moves toward the food along the axis with the larger gap, and when that move is immediately fatal it takes any non-fatal move, preferring straight.
90	  - Given food straight ahead and no danger ahead, when the scripted agent moves, then it goes straight.
91	  - Given food straight ahead and a wall ahead, when it moves, then it turns to a side that is free.
92	- **BASE-03** Both baselines are scored on the same 100 held-out games as the learning agent.
93	  - Given the 100 held-out seeds, when each baseline plays them, then each records one score per seed, and its mean and spread are logged.
94	- **BASE-04** In the tank, the scripted fish swims at full speed, turning as far as it can away from the ray that sees the ball closest; with no ball in reach it swims straight, and when the wall is within 1 unit straight ahead it turns as far as it can toward the side whose rays see more room.
95	  - Given the ball seen only on the front-left ray and no wall within 1 unit ahead, when the scripted fish moves, then it turns −15° (to the right) at speed 0.3.
96	  - Given no ball in reach and the wall 0.5 units straight ahead with more room on the left, when it moves, then it turns +15° at speed 0.3.
97	
98	- **BASE-05** The scripted predator swims at full speed, turning as far as it can toward the ray that sees a prey closest (not turning when that ray points straight ahead); with no prey in reach it swims straight, and when the wall is within 1 unit straight ahead it turns as far as it can toward the side whose rays see more room.
99	  - Given a prey seen only on the front-left ray, when the scripted predator moves, then it turns +10° (to the left) at speed 0.35.
100	  - Given no prey in reach and the wall 0.5 units straight ahead with more room on the right, when it moves, then it turns −10° at speed 0.35.
101	- **BASE-06** The scripted prey swims at full speed: when the wall is within 1 unit straight ahead it turns as far as it can toward the side whose rays see more room; otherwise, when the predator is in reach it turns as far as it can away from the ray that sees the predator closest; otherwise it turns as far as it can toward the ray that sees a pellet closest; with nothing in reach it swims straight.
102	  - Given the predator seen only on the front-left ray and no wall within 1 unit ahead, when the scripted prey moves, then it turns −15° at speed 0.3.
103	  - Given no predator in reach and a pellet seen only on the right ray, when it moves, then it turns −15° at speed 0.3.
104	
105	**Edge cases & errors**
106	- Every move is fatal → the scripted agent goes straight and the game ends.
107	- The scripted fish sees the ball equally close on two rays → it turns away from the one further left.
108	- The scripted fish sees the ball and the wall ahead at once → it avoids the wall.
109	- The scripted predator or prey sees its target equally close on two rays → it uses the one further left.
110	
111	### Evaluation (`EVAL`)
112	**Serves:** Goal 2
113	**Stage:** S1
114	**Behavior:** Every training run is checked at regular points on a fixed set of held-out games it never trains on, with exploration off, and ends with a final check on all 100 held-out games. Each learning agent's final check is compared with both baselines of its environment on those same games. In the predator-prey tank each side is checked against the other side's scripted opponent, and compared with the random and scripted versions of itself against that same opponent.
115	
116	**Requirements**
117	- **EVAL-01** Training games and evaluation games come from separate seed sets that never overlap.
118	  - Given any training run, when its training and evaluation seeds are listed, then no seed appears in both.
119	- **EVAL-02** During training the agent plays held-out games at regular points with exploration off and its mean score is logged: on Snake every 50 training games over all 100 held-out games; in the tank at every tenth of training over the same 20 of its 100 held-out games; in the predator-prey tank at every tenth of training, each side's current policy against the other side's scripted opponent over the same 20 of its 100 held-out games. Every run ends with a final check over all 100.
120	  - Given a Snake run of 1,000 training games, when it ends, then 20 evaluation results are logged, each over the same 100 seeds.
121	  - Given a tank run, when it ends, then 10 evaluation results over the same 20 seeds and one final result over all 100 seeds are logged.
122	  - Given a predator-prey run, when it ends, then for each side 10 evaluation results over the same 20 seeds and one final result over all 100 seeds are logged.
123	- **EVAL-03** Each learning agent's final evaluation scores are compared with each baseline's scores of its environment with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill, given 5 training seeds × the 100 held-out games for the agent and the same 100 games for each baseline; the agent beats a baseline only if its scores are higher at a 5% significance level after correcting for every comparison in that environment (2 on Snake: the full agent against 2 baselines; 6 in the tank: 3 rungs against 2 baselines; 12 in the predator-prey tank: 3 rungs × 2 sides against 2 baselines, 8 without the taking-turns rung). In the predator-prey tank, each rung's last predator generation plays the scripted prey, and is compared with the random and the scripted predator playing the scripted prey on the same games; each rung's last prey generation is compared the same way against the scripted predator.
124	  - Given finished runs of the full agent and both baselines, when the comparison is run, then the report says, for the random agent and for the scripted agent, whether the agent beats it, with the test result.
125	  - Given finished runs of all 3 rungs and both tank baselines, when the comparison is run, then the report says, for each of the 6 pairs, whether the rung beats the baseline, with the test result.
126	  - Given finished runs of the self-play rung and both baselines of each side, when the comparison is run, then the report says, for the predator against the random and the scripted predator, and for the prey against the random and the scripted prey, whether the learned side beats it, with the test result.
127	- **EVAL-04** On Snake the full agent's learning curve beats both the random baseline and the scripted baseline; in the tank every rung's learning curve beats both the random fish and the scripted fish; in the predator-prey tank the self-play rung's predator beats both the random and the scripted predator, and its prey beats both the random and the scripted prey.
128	  - Given the EVAL-03 report for Snake, when it is read, then the agent beats both baselines.
129	  - Given the EVAL-03 report for the tank, when it is read, then each of the 3 rungs beats both baselines.
130	  - Given the EVAL-03 report for the predator-prey tank, when it is read, then the self-play rung's predator and prey each beat both baselines of their side.
131	
132	**Edge cases & errors**
133	- A run stops early → its completed evaluation points are kept and shown; it is left out of the final comparison, which names it as incomplete.
134	
135	### Run logging (`LOG`)
136	**Serves:** Goal 2, Goal 4
137	**Stage:** S1
138	**Behavior:** Every run, whether training or baseline, keeps a record of its settings and results, so curves can be redrawn and agents replayed without training again.
139	
140	**Requirements**
141	- **LOG-01** Each run records its environment, its agent (each side's, where two sides learn), every setting including the ablation switches and the rung, and its seed.
142	  - Given a finished run, when its record is opened, then the environment, the agent, every setting and the seed are listed.
143	- **LOG-02** Each run records the score and length of every training game, with each side's score where there are two.
144	  - Given a run of 1,000 games, when its record is read, then it holds 1,000 scores and game lengths.
145	  - Given a predator-prey run of 1,000 games, when its record is read, then it holds 1,000 predator scores, 1,000 prey scores and 1,000 game lengths.
146	- **LOG-03** Each run records every evaluation result and the training loss every 50 games.
147	  - Given a finished run, when its record is read, then each evaluation point has its mean score and spread, and each 50-game point has its average loss.
148	- **LOG-04** Each training run saves its final trained agent next to its record; a predator-prey run saves every generation of each side (SELF-02).
149	  - Given a finished training run, when its record is opened, then the saved agent can be loaded (AGENT-06).
150	  - Given a finished predator-prey run, when its record is opened, then each of its 20 saved generations can be loaded (SELF-07).
151	- **LOG-05** A run's record is kept on disk while the run is in progress, at least after every evaluation point.
152	  - Given a run stopped at game 430, when its record is opened, then results up to game 400 are present.
153	
154	**Edge cases & errors**
155	- Starting a run with the same name as an existing one → refused, naming the existing run; nothing is overwritten.
156	
157	### Learning curves (`CURVE`)
158	**Serves:** Goal 2, Goal 1
159	**Stage:** S1
160	**Behavior:** From saved run records, the learner draws learning curves: evaluation score against training games, with the environment's baselines as flat reference lines. Curves for several runs can share one chart, so the full agent and its ablations, or the three rungs of a ladder, can be compared at a glance. In the predator-prey tank each side has its own chart.
161	
162	**Requirements**
163	- **CURVE-01** A learning curve shows the mean evaluation score against training games for one or more runs.
164	  - Given one run's record, when its curve is drawn, then the chart shows one line of mean evaluation score at each evaluation point.
165	- **CURVE-02** Runs of the same agent and settings with different seeds are drawn as one line of their mean, with a band showing their spread.
166	  - Given 5 seeds of the full agent, when drawn, then the chart shows one mean line and one band.
167	- **CURVE-03** The random and scripted baselines' mean scores of the chart's environment appear on every chart as labelled flat lines.
168	  - Given any Snake chart, when drawn, then two labelled flat lines show the random and scripted baselines' mean scores.
169	  - Given any tank chart, when drawn, then two labelled flat lines show the random and scripted fish's mean scores.
170	  - Given a predator-prey chart of the predator, when drawn, then two labelled flat lines show the random and the scripted predator's mean scores against the scripted prey; a chart of the prey shows the random and the scripted prey's against the scripted predator.
171	- **CURVE-04** The full agent and each ablated agent can be drawn together on one chart, each labelled by its switches.
172	  - Given the full agent and 3 ablation runs, when drawn together, then 4 labelled lines appear.
173	- **CURVE-05** Curves are drawn from saved records only, never by training again.
174	  - Given saved records and no training running, when a chart is drawn, then it appears in ≤ 10 seconds.
175	- **CURVE-06** The 3 rungs of the ladder can be drawn together on one chart, each labelled by its rung.
176	  - Given 5 seeds of each rung, when drawn together, then 3 labelled mean lines with bands appear.
177	- **CURVE-07** The rungs of the self-play ladder can be drawn together, one chart per side, each line labelled by its rung.
178	  - Given 5 seeds of each of the 3 self-play rungs, when drawn, then a predator chart and a prey chart each show 3 labelled mean lines with bands.
179	
180	**Edge cases & errors**
181	- A record has no evaluation points yet → it is skipped with a note naming it; the other curves are drawn.
182	- Records from different environments are drawn together → refused, naming the environments.
183	
184	### Replay viewer (`REPLAY`)
185	**Serves:** Goal 3
186	**Stage:** S1
187	**Behavior:** The learner picks any saved agent, or a baseline, and a seed, and watches it play that game frame by frame in a simple view of the environment, with pause, single-step and speed controls.
188	
189	**Requirements**
190	- **REPLAY-01** The learner can watch any saved trained agent, the random agent or the scripted agent play a game from a chosen seed; in the predator-prey tank the learner picks one of these for each side, a saved generation counting as a saved agent.
191	  - Given a saved agent and seed 42, when a replay is requested, then the agent's game on seed 42 is shown from the first frame to game over.
192	  - Given predator generation 10, prey generation 3 and seed 42, when a replay is requested, then their game on seed 42 is shown from the first frame to game over.
193	- **REPLAY-02** Each frame shows the environment's drawing (CORE-04) plus the step number and score.
194	  - Given a Snake replay, when any frame is shown, then the grid, snake, food, step number and score are visible.
195	  - Given a tank replay, when any frame is shown, then the tank, the ball, each living fish with its heading, the number of fish left and the step number are visible.
196	  - Given a predator-prey replay, when any frame is shown, then the tank, the pellets, the predator with its heading, each living prey with its heading and energy, the number of prey left and the step number are visible.
197	- **REPLAY-03** The learner can pause, step one frame forward and change the playback speed between 1 and 30 frames per second.
198	  - Given a playing replay, when paused and stepped, then exactly one frame advances; when the speed is set to 30, then 30 frames show per second.
199	- **REPLAY-04** Replaying the same agent on the same seed shows the identical game every time.
200	  - Given a saved agent and seed 42, when replayed twice, then every frame and the final score match.
201	- **REPLAY-05** The final score of a replayed game matches that game's score in the agent's evaluation.
202	  - Given a held-out seed scored in evaluation, when it is replayed with the same saved agent, then the replay's final score equals the logged score.
203	
204	**Edge cases & errors**
205	- The saved agent doesn't exist or can't be loaded → an error names it; no window opens.
206	- The game never ends → it ends by the environment's limit (SNAKE-05, TANK-06), so every replay has a last frame.
207	
208	### Explanation notes (`EXPL`)
209	**Serves:** Goal 1
210	**Stage:** S1
211	**Behavior:** Each environment has a notes page, written by the learner in their own words, explaining why the agent learns, what each ablated part does on Snake and in the tank, in the tank what each rung of the ladder fixes, and in the predator-prey tank why self-play works and what each rung of its ladder shows, next to the evidence from that environment's study.
212	
213	**Requirements**
214	- **EXPL-01** Each environment has a notes page with a section explaining why the agent learns there.
215	  - Given Snake is finished, when its notes page is opened, then it has a "Why it learns" section written by the learner.
216	- **EXPL-02** The notes page has one section per ablation switch, giving that ablation's study result and the learner's explanation of it.
217	  - Given the ablation study report, when the notes page is read, then each of the 3 switches has a section with its result (ABL-04) and an explanation.
218	- **EXPL-03** The notes page includes the learning-curve charts comparing the full agent, its ablations and the baselines; in the tank also the 3 rungs and the baselines; in the predator-prey tank the rungs of each side against that side's baselines, and each rung's cross-play table.
219	  - Given the Snake notes page, when opened, then the CURVE-04 chart with the CURVE-03 baseline lines is shown on it.
220	  - Given the tank notes page, when opened, then the CURVE-04 chart and the CURVE-06 chart, each with the CURVE-03 baseline lines, are shown on it.
221	  - Given the predator-prey notes page, when opened, then the two CURVE-07 charts, each with the CURVE-03 baseline lines, and each rung's cross-play table (CROSS-02) are shown on it.
222	- **EXPL-04** The tank's notes page has one section per rung above the first, saying which failure of the rung below it fixes, with its ladder result (PG-10).
223	  - Given the ladder report, when the tank notes page is read, then rungs 2 and 3 each have a section with their result and the learner's explanation.
224	- **EXPL-05** The predator-prey notes page has a "Why self-play works" section and one section per rung, giving its baseline result (EVAL-03) and its cross-play result (CROSS-03), and saying whether the simultaneous learners cycled or stalled where self-play did not, or recording that they didn't.
225	  - Given the baseline and cross-play reports, when the predator-prey notes page is read, then it has a "Why self-play works" section, and each finished rung has a section with both results and the learner's explanation.
226	
227	**Edge cases & errors**
228	- An ablation shows no detectable difference → its section still exists and explains why that part may not matter in that environment.
229	- A rung shows no detectable difference from the one below → its section still exists and explains why the fix may not matter in the tank.
230	- The simultaneous learners show no cycling or stalling → the notes record that, with the cross-play table as evidence.
231	
232	### Discrete tank (`TWIN`)
233	**Serves:** Goal 1
234	**Stage:** S2
235	**Source:** [ideas/fish-tank.md](ideas/fish-tank.md)
236	**Behavior:** An optional twin of the fish tank where each fish picks one of 8 compass moves at full speed instead of steering continuously, trained by the unchanged value-learning agent, so the learner can see what continuous control adds. S2 is complete without it.
237	
238	**Requirements**
239	- **TWIN-01** The discrete tank is the fish tank with each fish choosing one of 8 compass directions (north, north-east, …, north-west) each step and swimming 0.3 units that way; everything else is as in the fish tank.
240	  - Given a fish at the centre, when it picks north-east, then it ends 0.3 units north-east of the centre, heading north-east.
241	- **TWIN-02** The value-learning agent trains on the discrete tank with no change to how it learns, treating each fish's steps as its own remembered steps.
242	  - Given the discrete tank with 5 fish, when the value-learning agent trains on it, then training completes, and its run is logged and drawn against the tank's baselines.
243	
244	**Edge cases & errors**
245	- The discrete tank is left unbuilt → nothing else in S2 depends on it.
246	
247	## Quality requirements
248	- **QR-01** Every training run of a learning agent finishes in under ~1 hour (≤ 60 minutes) on the learner's laptop CPU: the value-learning agent on Snake; each rung and ablation in the tank with 5 fish; and each rung in the predator-prey tank with 5 prey, where one run is the whole run, every generation of both sides.
249	  - Timed on the learner's laptop with no GPU, for the default number of training games.
250	- **QR-02** Each environment's full study runs unattended to completion with one command: Snake's (full agent and 3 ablations × 5 seeds, plus baselines) in ≤ 10 hours; the tank's (3 rungs and 3 ablations × 5 seeds, plus baselines) in ≤ 15 hours; the predator-prey tank's (3 rungs × 5 seeds, plus both sides' baselines and every run's cross-play table) in ≤ 16 hours, or ≤ 11 hours without the taking-turns rung.
251	  - Started once on the learner's laptop, it finishes without intervention; total time read from the run records.
252	- **QR-03** Every learning algorithm is written by hand: the value targets, remembered steps, frozen copy, exploration and update step of the value-learning agent, the move spreads, returns, value estimate, advantages, update cap and update step of the ladder, and the generation saving, frozen side and opponent mix of the self-play ladder; no reinforcement-learning library is used. General array math with automatic gradients is allowed.
253	  - Checked by reading the project's dependency list and the agents' code.
254	- **QR-04** The lab uses only free tools and needs no paid service or account.
255	  - Checked by reading the dependency list: every item is free to use.
256	- **QR-05** On the same machine, a training run repeated with the same seed and settings produces the same logged results.
257	  - Two runs of 200 games with seed 1 give identical evaluation scores.
258	
259	## Assumptions
260	- The learner is the only user; the lab runs on their laptop.
261	- Tiny test environments exist only to prove the shared core; they are not lab environments.
262	- Snake: 20 × 20 grid, starting length 3, step limit 100 × length, rewards +10 / −10 / 0.
263	- Value-learning agent settings: exploration 1.0 → 0.01 over half the games, memory of 100,000 steps, discount 0.9, frozen copy refreshed every 1,000 steps, fixed 0.1 exploration when decay is off.
264	- Study size: 5 training seeds per agent, 100 held-out games; Snake evaluates every 50 games, the tank at every tenth of training on 20 games plus a final 100; Snake's study ≤ 10 hours.
265	- The scripted baseline avoids immediately fatal moves; the agent may only tie it, and that bar is kept on purpose.
266	- Tank: radius 10, ball radius 1 at 0.25 per step, fish radius 0.3, 5 fish by default (1–10), 1,000-step limit; turn −15° to +15° then swim 0 to 0.3 per step.
267	- A fish senses 8 rays of reach 4 (wall, ball, nearest other fish) plus its own speed, and doesn't sense the ball's motion; the bet is that the rays are enough to dodge.
268	- Tank reward is +1 per step alive, with no extra penalty when hit; the scripted fish flees the ball and steers clear of the wall, a tougher bar kept on purpose.
269	- Ladder settings: discount 0.99, blend 0.95, one update per game, 10 passes in batches of 64, a 20% cap; the tank's switches apply to rung 3.
270	- Learning "more steadily" is reported as the spread across seeds, not tested; the tank's study is ≤ 15 hours.
271	- The discrete tank is optional; S2 is complete without it.
272	- Predator-prey tank: radius 10, 5 prey by default (1–10) moving as S2's fish, one predator of radius 0.5 turning −10° to +10° and swimming 0 to 0.35 per step (faster but less agile), catching at < 0.8; 1,000-step limit. Only prey have energy: 1.0 at the start, −0.002 per step, +0.3 per pellet up to 1.0. 10 pellets of radius 0.2 are always present, and a contested pellet goes to the closer prey.
273	- Prey see 34 numbers (wall, predator, nearest other prey and nearest pellet on 8 rays of reach 4, plus speed and energy); the predator sees 17 (wall and nearest prey on 8 rays, plus speed). Prey earn +1 per step alive; the predator +1 per catch.
274	- Self-play settings: 10 generations per side per run, one at every tenth of training; games are 50% current against current, 25% against a past prey generation and 25% against a past predator generation, each past one drawn uniformly. Taking turns is 10 alternating blocks, prey first, and is optional.
275	- Only the self-play rung must beat both baselines of each side and show later generations beating earlier ones; the other rungs are reported. The cross-play table is 10 × 10 over 20 held-out games; the predator-prey study is ≤ 16 hours, or ≤ 11 without taking turns.
276

# rllab — Pipelines

**Package:** rllab · **Status:** Approved · **Last updated:** 2026-10-19 · **Contract:** [contract.md](contract.md)

## PL-01: Train an agent
**Entry:** `src/rllab/cli/pipeline.py::run_train` · **Trigger:** `python -m rllab train --env snake --seed 1 [--agent dqn|pg] [--rung 1|2|3] [--no-memory] [--no-frozen] [--no-decay] [--no-value] [--no-cap] [--no-rescale] [--fish 5] [--compass] [--prey 5] [--selfplay turns|simultaneous|selfplay] [--generations 10] [--games 1000] [--name NAME]`
**Purpose:** Train one hand-written learner (the DQN, or a rung of the policy-gradient ladder) on an environment, with any of its parts switched off, or train both sides of a two-sided environment against each other by taking turns, learning at once or self-play, and keep the record, each side's trained agent and its generations in `runs/<env>/<name>/`.

5	## PL-01: Train an agent
6	**Entry:** `src/rllab/cli/pipeline.py::run_train` · **Trigger:** `python -m rllab train --env snake --seed 1 [--agent dqn|pg] [--rung 1|2|3] [--no-memory] [--no-frozen] [--no-decay] [--no-value] [--no-cap] [--no-rescale] [--fish 5] [--compass] [--prey 5] [--selfplay turns|simultaneous|selfplay] [--generations 10] [--games 1000] [--name NAME]`
7	**Purpose:** Train one hand-written learner (the DQN, or a rung of the policy-gradient ladder) on an environment, with any of its parts switched off, or train both sides of a two-sided environment against each other by taking turns, learning at once or self-play, and keep the record, each side's trained agent and its generations in `runs/<env>/<name>/`.
8	**Covers:** CORE-01–CORE-10, SNAKE-01–SNAKE-08, TANK-01–TANK-10, HUNT-01–HUNT-12, TWIN-01, TWIN-02, AGENT-01–AGENT-06, PG-01–PG-09, PG-12, SELF-01–SELF-05, SELF-07, ABL-01–ABL-03, ABL-06–ABL-08, EVAL-01, EVAL-02, LOG-01–LOG-05, QR-01, QR-03–QR-05

12	| 1 | `train_config` | cli.args | flags → RunConfig | `agents` keyed by the environment's side names from a plain match (`snake`; `walker`; `fish`; `point`; `predator` and `prey`; `chaser` and `runner`): on a one-sided environment `--agent dqn` switches into AgentConfig, `--agent pg` rung and switches into PGConfig; on the hunt both sides `AgentSpec("pg", PGConfig(rung=3))`, `--agent`, `--rung` or a switch refused naming it; on the chase both sides `pg` at `--rung`; a Snake switch with `pg` or a tank switch with `dqn` refused, naming the switch and the agent; `--fish`/`--compass`/`--prey` into `env_options`; on a two-sided environment `selfplay` from `--selfplay` (default `selfplay` on the hunt, `simultaneous` on the chase; `first` `prey` on the hunt, `chaser` on the chase) and `generations` from `--generations` (default 10, else 0); cadence from `eval_schedule`; default name `<variant>-s<seed>`, the variant a self-play rung's name on a two-sided environment | LOG-01, ABL-01–ABL-03, ABL-06–ABL-08, SELF-01, SELF-02 |

19	| 8 | `HuntEnv` | hunt.env | prey → Env | NumPy rules in HUNT's order (AD-21): swim by `arena.swim` (limits 9.5 and 9.7), catch under 0.8, eat within 0.5 (closer prey, then lower id) with the respawn by `arena.random_spots` at least 0.5 from living prey, energy in thousandths (−2, +300, cap 1,000) and starving at 0; 10 pellets; the 1,000-step limit; rewards and scores per HUNT-10 and HUNT-11; observations from `arena`'s rays (34 per prey, 17 for the predator); frame with wall, pellets, headings and energy bars; sides `predator` (id 0) and `prey` (ids 1–n) | HUNT-01–HUNT-12, CORE-02–CORE-04 |

25	| 14 | `ChaseEnv` | chase.env | length, max steps → Env | chaser at 0, runner at `length`, both pushing within `[0, length]`; a touch within 0.5 gives the chaser +1 and ends the game, else the runner +1; ends at `max_steps`; sides `chaser` (id 0) and `runner` (id 1) | CORE-10 |
26	| 15 | `make_agent` | registry.learners | RunConfig, Env, side → LearningAgent | plain match on the side's `AgentSpec.kind`: `dqn` → `DQNAgent`, `pg` → `PGAgent` on that side's size and moves, then `check_agent` for that side | CORE-07, SELF-01, ABL-01–ABL-03, ABL-06–ABL-08 |
27	| 16 | `DQNAgent` | agent.dqn | obs size, choice count, AgentConfig, seed → LearningAgent | 11 → 256 → 3 MLP learning in `learn` from the latest Transition only, ε fixed at 0.1, targets from the current net (every switch off); `end_game` returns `None`; `torch.save` state dict | AGENT-01–AGENT-06, ABL-01–ABL-03, TWIN-02, QR-03, QR-04 |
28	| 17 | `PGAgent` | pg.agent | obs size, Controls, PGConfig, seed → LearningAgent | rung 1 for every rung: obs → 64 → 64 → centres MLP with a learned log width, normal draws clipped to the limits, centre when greedy; `learn` stores the step; `end_game` weights each step's log-probability by the discounted return (0.99) to that fish's end and makes one Adam step; `torch.save` state dict; `load` checks the side and seeds its draws (AD-13, AD-22) | PG-01–PG-06, PG-12, SELF-01, SELF-07, QR-03 |
29	| 18 | `make_schedule` | selfplay.schedule | SelfPlayConfig, learners, n games, seed, loader → Schedule | plain match on `config.rung`; refuses anything but 2 sides, a `first` that isn't one, or `blocks` not dividing `n_games` | SELF-03–SELF-05 |
30	| 19 | `TurnsSchedule` | selfplay.turns | SelfPlayConfig, learners, n games → Schedule | game g in block `g · blocks // n_games`, block 0 learning `first`, sides alternating, the other side's current learner playing frozen; `add_generation` ignored | SELF-03, QR-03 |
31	| 20 | `SimultaneousSchedule` | selfplay.simultaneous | learners → Schedule | every game both current learners, both learning; `add_generation` ignored | SELF-04, QR-03 |
32	| 21 | `PoolSchedule` | selfplay.pool | SelfPlayConfig, learners, seed, loader → Schedule | current against current while the opponent has no generation; else a kind drawn with shares 0.5, 0.25, 0.25 and a past generation uniformly from those added, from a generator built from the run seed; each past generation loaded once by the loader and cached, playing frozen (AD-22) | SELF-05, QR-03, QR-05 |
33	| 22 | `load_agent` | registry.agents | agent spec, Env, side → Agent | `random` → `RandomAgent`; `scripted` → `ScriptedAgent` on Snake, `ScriptedFish` on the continuous tank, `ScriptedPredator` or `ScriptedPrey` on the hunt's sides, refused elsewhere naming the environment and side; a run folder via its record's `AgentSpec` and `DQNAgent.load` or `PGAgent.load` at `agent_path`, `<run>@<n>` its generation n | SELF-07, REPLAY-01 |
34	| 23 | `RandomAgent` | baselines.uniform | name → Agent | uniform over `range(n)` for Choices, each control uniform within its limits for Controls, reading the side of the id asked; generator reseeded in `reset` | BASE-01 |
35	| 24 | `ScriptedPredator` | baselines.predator | name → Agent | from the rays only: speed 0.35; a prey in reach → turn 10° toward the ray seeing it closest (ray 0 straight, ray 4 left); else the wall within 1 unit ahead → 10° toward more room, left on a tie; else straight | BASE-05 |
36	| 25 | `ScriptedPrey` | baselines.prey | name → Agent | from the rays only: speed 0.3; the wall within 1 unit ahead → 15° toward more room; else away from the ray seeing the predator closest; else toward the ray seeing a pellet closest; else straight | BASE-06 |
37	| 26 | `train_run` | train.loop | Env, LearningAgent, RunConfig, Lineup, Schedule → RunRecord | game loop: each game `schedule.match(game)`, or every learner learning with no schedule; `begin_game` for learning sides; `act(explore=True)` per living id in id order through its side's agent, `step`, `learn` per learning side's Transition in id order, `end_game` for learning sides (AD-12, AD-20); every `eval_every` games `evaluate` each learning side on the first `eval_games` held-out seeds against `opponents` for the other sides, plus each side's mean loss; every `n_games / generations` games `save_generation` per learning side and `add_generation`; the final check over all 100 per side; each learner saved; failed record on any error, generations kept | CORE-05, CORE-07–CORE-10, AGENT-02, EVAL-02, LOG-02, LOG-03, SELF-02, QR-01, QR-05 |
